5 
Q.
By whom are you employed and in what capacity? 
6

7 
of National Grid USA (“National Grid”), and currently hold the position of 
8 
Director, NY Environmental.  My responsibilities include overseeing the 
9 
environmental and site investigation and remediation (“SIR”) program 
10 
along with other environmental programs for National Grid’s New York 
11 
operating companies, including The Brooklyn Union Gas Company d/b/a 
12

13 
Corporation d/b/a National Grid (“KEDLI”).

16

17

18 
Engineering Geology with a concentration in Environmental Engineering 
19 
from Drexel University and a Master’s in Business Administration from 
20 
LeMoyne University.

1

2

3 
National Grid, I held various management level positions in the field of 
4

5

6

7

II. 
SIR Program Overview 
7 
Q. 
Please provide a brief overview of KEDNY’s SIR program.  
8 
A.  
KEDNY’s SIR program includes activities in connection with the 
9 
management, investigation, and remediation of MGP and Superfund sites 
10 
that have been contaminated by the past release of substances from property 
11 
owned or formerly owned by KEDNY or its predecessors.  KEDNY has 
12 
responsibility for 27 MGP and two federal Superfund sites associated with 
13 
waterways (Gowanus Canal and Newtown Creek) adjacent to former MGP 
14 
sites.  The Gowanus Canal is in active remedial construction and Newtown 
15 
Creek is in the remedial planning process.  Of the 27 MGP sites, four sites 
16 
are in site characterization or remedial investigation, three sites are in 
17 
remedial planning, three sites are in remedial action, one inactive site is 
18 
being monitored and 12 sites have been either remediated but are subject to 
19 
future monitoring and/or are subject to DEC site management plan 
20 
obligations.  Four sites have received a “No Further Action” determination 
21 


--- Page 8 ---
Testimony of Charles F. Willard 
Page 6 of 40 
following site characterization and/or remediation.  As I will discuss later 
1 
in my testimony, remediation and investigation activities at the Gowanus 
2 
Canal and Newtown Creek Superfund sites are projected to cause KEDNY 
3 
to incur significant SIR costs in the Rate Year, Data Years, and beyond.  
4 
5 
Exhibit __ (CFW-1) describes the sites currently being managed by 
6 
KEDNY.  The exhibit includes the background of each site and details the 
7 
investigation and remediation activities that have taken place over the last 
8 
three years from January 2019 through the end of the Historic Test Year.  
9 
10 
Q.
What types of costs does KEDNY incur under its SIR program?  
11 
A.
Costs under KEDNY’s SIR program include consultant and contractor 
12 
costs, remediation activities aimed at reducing the volume, toxicity, or 
13 
mobility of pre-existing contamination, and incremental external costs 
14 
(including insurance and legal costs) incurred to seek recovery from third 
15 
parties or to otherwise mitigate the Company’s SIR costs or liabilities. 
16 
17 
Q.
What roles do the DEC and EPA play regarding the scope and timing 
18 
of investigation and remediation work conducted at MGP and 
19 
Superfund sites?  
20 
A.
The DEC and EPA control the scope and timing of work at MGP and 
21 


--- Page 9 ---
Testimony of Charles F. Willard 
Page 7 of 40 
Superfund sites.  The scope of KEDNY’s site investigations, work plans, 
1 
clean-up, and field work decisions are reviewed, approved, and/or expanded 
2 
by the DEC and/or the EPA pursuant to various orders.  An example Order 
3 
on Consent for 23 of KEDNY’s MGP sites is provided in Exhibit __ (CFW-
4 
2).  The EPA Unilateral Administrative Orders for the remedial design, 
5 
remedial action of Remedial Target Area (“RTA”) 1, and remedial design 
6 
of RTAs-2 and 3 of the Gowanus Canal Superfund site and the 
7 
Administrative Settlement Agreement and EPA Order on Consent for the 
8 
remedial investigation and feasibility study of the Newtown Creek 
9 
Superfund site are provided in Exhibit __ (CFW-3) and Exhibit __ (CFW-
10 
4), respectively.  Although the Company has limited control over the 
11 
scope and timing of SIR activities, KEDNY challenges the DEC and EPA 
12 
when a more cost effective and equally protective remedy is available and, 
13 
as described later in my testimony, manages costs in the areas it can control, 
14 
such as contracting procedures.  Exhibit __ (CFW-5) provides examples 
15 
of changes to DEC and EPA remedy decisions and value engineering-
16 
related modifications following discussions with KEDNY that resulted in 
17 
cost savings for customers. 
18 
 
19 
Q.  
How is the schedule for work at MGP sites determined?  
20 
A.  
Prior to the beginning of each New York State fiscal year (April 1 through 
21 


--- Page 10 ---
Testimony of Charles F. Willard 
Page 8 of 40 
March 31), KEDNY and the DEC meet to discuss the upcoming work 
1 
schedule.  The DEC approves the work schedule for the upcoming fiscal 
2 
year only.  As a result, the amount of spending in a given year is highly 
3 
dependent upon the DEC and other third parties, including private property 
4 
owners and permitting authorities.  While the DEC only approves a one-
5 
year schedule, the Company and the DEC discuss the anticipated logical 
6 
progression of work at each site for an additional year beyond the upcoming 
7 
fiscal year, which gives the Company a sense of the projected work that will 
8 
be required.  
9 
 
10 
The DEC-approved schedule for the twelve months ended March 31, 2022, 
11 
March 31, 2023 and March 31, 2024 is provided in Exhibit __ (CFW-6).  
12 
The schedule includes KEDNY’s 23 actively tracked MGP sites subject to 
13 
DEC Orders on Consent. Three sites (Jamaica Holder Station, Keap Street 
14 
Holder Station, and Rutledge Street Holder Station) have received “No 
15 
Further Action” determinations and are no longer tracked with the DEC.  
16 
Remediation at a fourth site, the Newtown Holder Station, was previously 
17 
completed and the site closed in approximately 2002.  
18 
 
19 
The schedule for work at the Gowanus Canal and Newtown Creek 
20 
Superfund sites as required by the EPA is discussed later in my testimony.  
21 


--- Page 11 ---
Testimony of Charles F. Willard 
Page 9 of 40 
Q. 
Has the Company prepared an exhibit setting forth the status of its 
1 
remediation process in relation to existing timetables and DEC and 
2 
EPA requirements?  
3 
A.  
Yes.  Exhibit __ (CFW-7) shows the status as of the end of calendar year 
4 
2022 for each site, indicates whether there was a difference between the 
5 
scheduled activities and the activities actually completed and, if there was 
6 
a difference, explains the reason.  Variances from the timetable have 
7 
occurred at two of KEDNY’s MGP sites and were due to delays resulting 
8 
from site access constraints, and regulatory reviews or approvals, all of 
9 
which were outside of KEDNY’s control.  
10 
 
11 
Q.  
Has KEDNY discussed the differences identified in Exhibit __ (CFW-
12 
7) with the DEC and EPA?  
13 
A.  
Yes.  KEDNY updates the DEC quarterly and the EPA, monthly, on the 
14 
status of each site.  Further, the Company’s project managers 
15 
communicate with the DEC’s and EPA’s project managers on project 
16 
schedule and progress of work as necessary.  Based on these updates, it is 
17 
my understanding that the DEC and EPA are satisfied with KEDNY’s 
18 
progress.  
19

Page 10 of 40 
III.  
SIR Program Costs 
1 
Q.  
What level of SIR costs does KEDNY currently recover annually 
2 
through base rates?  
3 
A.  
KEDNY’s rate allowance currently includes: (i) historic, unrecovered SIR 
4 
expenditures in an amount equal to one-tenth of the forecast SIR deferral 
5 
balance as of December 31, 2016 ($18.521 million per year); and (ii) 
6 
average forecast SIR costs of $70.92 million in FY 2021 – FY 2024) (the 
7 
“Forecast Rate Allowance”).  These amounts were approved in the 
8 
Commission’s August 12, 2021 Order in the 2019 KEDNY and KEDLI 
9 
Rate Cases.   
10 
 
11 
Because of the dynamic scope of the Gowanus Canal and Newtown Creek 
12 
projects and the number of parties involved, it was difficult to predict the 
13 
precise amount of these costs in the 2019 KEDNY and KEDLI Rate Cases.  
14 
Consequently, the Forecast Rate Allowance reflected $20 million in each of 
15 
the Rate Year and Data Years for costs associated with remediation of 
16 
Gowanus Canal and Newtown Creek, based on the combined spending on 
17 
these sites during the Historic Test Year.   
18 
 
19 
 
20 
 
21 


--- Page 13 ---
Testimony of Charles F. Willard 
Page 11 of 40 
Q. 
Does KEDNY fully reconcile SIR costs? 
1 
A. 
Yes.  Each year, KEDNY fully reconciles its actual SIR expense (inclusive 
2 
of Gowanus Canal and Newtown Creek costs) to the Forecast Rate 
3 
Allowance.  Any under or over expenditures are deferred for future refund 
4 
to or recovery from customers (with the exception of the Citizens site).  
5 
KEDNY agreed to absorb 10 percent of the remaining investigation costs 
6 
for the Citizens site pursuant to the “Stipulation and Agreement Resolving 
7 
Corporate Structure Issues and Establishing Multi-Year Rate Plan,” dated 
8 
June 25, 1996 in Case 95-G-0671. 
9 
 
10 
Additionally, given the uncertain timing and potential magnitude of SIR 
11 
costs, the 2016 and 2019 Joint Proposals in the 2016 KEDNY and KEDLI 
12 
Rate Cases and the 2019 KEDNY and KEDLI Rate Cases authorized 
13 
KEDNY to implement an SIR Recovery Surcharge. 
14 
 
15 
Q. 
Please explain how the SIR Recovery Surcharge operates. 
16 
A. 
Beginning in calendar year 2018, if the difference between actual SIR 
17 
expense (inclusive of Gowanus Canal and Newtown Creek costs) and the 
18 
Forecast Rate Allowance exceeds $25 million on a cumulative basis, 
19 
KEDNY is authorized to recover through the SIR Recovery Surcharge: (i) 
20 
the difference between actual SIR expense and the Forecast Rate 
21 


--- Page 14 ---
Testimony of Charles F. Willard 
Page 12 of 40 
Allowance, and (ii) any amount that was not recovered in the prior rate 
1 
year’s SIR Recovery Surcharge (if triggered) because the cumulative 
2 
difference between actual SIR costs and the Forecast Rate Allowance did 
3 
not exceed the $25 million threshold and/or the amount would have 
4 
increased KEDNY’s aggregate revenues by more than two percent.   
5 
 
6 
To date, KEDNY has not implemented the SIR Recovery Surcharge.  
7 
However, as explained below, given the size of the costs associated with 
8 
Gowanus Canal and Newtown Creek, the same circumstances that 
9 
supported Commission approval of KEDNY’s SIR Recovery Surcharge still 
10 
exist and warrant continuation of the mechanism. 
11 
 
12 
Q.  
What have the Company’s historic SIR costs been?  
13 
A. 
Prior to the Historic Test Year, during calendar years 2019 through 2021, 
14 
KEDNY 
conducted 
investigation, 
remediation, 
and 
operations, 
15 
maintenance, and monitoring (“OM&M”) activities pursuant to Orders on 
16 
Consent or EPA Administrative Agreements under the DEC’s and EPA’s 
17 
remedial programs at 23 of its MGP and Superfund sites.  KEDNY 
18 
incurred approximately $42.173 million, $152.957 million, and $77.689 
19 
million of SIR costs in calendar years 2019, 2020 and 2021, respectively, 
20 
for these activities.  While investigation and OM&M activities contributed 
21 


--- Page 15 ---
Testimony of Charles F. Willard 
Page 13 of 40 
to a steady and significant level of costs during this time, increased spending 
1 
was primarily due to the remedial construction activities at the Citizens 
2 
MGP site, Fulton MGP site, and Gowanus Canal Superfund site.   
3 
 
4 
Q.  
What SIR costs did KEDNY incur in the Historic Test Year?  
5 
A.  
KEDNY incurred $76.080 million in SIR costs in the Historic Test Year, as 
6 
shown in Exhibit __ (CFW-9).  Of this amount, $37.829 million was 
7 
incurred for design work and RTA-1 remedial construction at Gowanus 
8 
Canal, and $6.573 million for remedial investigation activities at Newtown 
9 
Creek (totaling $44.403 million).  The remaining $31.678 million was for 
10 
investigation and remediation activities at 23 of KEDNY’s MGP sites.  
11 
These activities included: remedial planning, remedial action, or OM&M 
12 
activities at 19 sites; site characterization or remedial investigation activities 
13 
at four sites; and SIR Program activities.  
14 
 
15 
Q.  
What is KEDNY’s forecast of SIR costs for work at the Company’s 
16 
MGP sites?  
17 
A.  
As shown in Exhibit __ (CFW-10), KEDNY forecasts SIR costs for work 
18 
at MGP sites to be $58.100 million in the Rate Year and $49.618 million, 
19 
$61.858 million, and $70.441 million in Data Years 1, 2 and 3, respectively.  
20 
These forecasts do not include projected costs for Gowanus Canal or 
21 


--- Page 16 ---
Testimony of Charles F. Willard 
Page 14 of 40 
Newtown Creek, which are addressed separately below.  
1 
 
2 
Q.  
Please explain how KEDNY developed its forecast of SIR costs for work 
3 
at its MGP sites.  
4 
A.  
The DEC schedule is not yet available for the Rate Year or Data Years. 
5 
Therefore, to develop the forecast, KEDNY utilized the current approved 
6 
DEC schedule as well as the anticipated work schedule for FY 2024.  
7 
KEDNY considered the status of each site and knowledge derived from 
8 
project managers regarding site progress and potential delays to determine 
9 
the projected work on a site-by-site basis.  KEDNY projected costs for 
10 
each site based on the nature of the work at the site and the expected remedy 
11 
for those sites where a remedy has been selected (e.g., excavation of site 
12 
soils, containment recovery, installation of barrier walls, treatment of 
13 
groundwater, etc.), site specific conditions, and past spending and estimates 
14 
provided by consultants for the anticipated work.  In instances where the 
15 
project manager anticipated delays (e.g., potential site access issues, 
16 
permitting delays, or onsite condition constraints), the timing of the work 
17 
was adjusted in the forecast.   
18 
 
19 
As shown in Exhibit __ (CFW-10), following the development of the MGP 
20 
site forecasts, the total MGP site forecast was reduced by 20 percent to 
21 


--- Page 17 ---
Testimony of Charles F. Willard 
Page 15 of 40 
account for unforeseen delays.  This reduction was based on the average 
1 
underspend over the past three calendar years.  The forecast for the 
2 
Gowanus and Newtown sites is the average of the past spend over the past 
3 
three calendar years (with a three percent inflation factor applied). 
4 
 
5 
Q.  
What are the major drivers of SIR costs in the Rate Year for KEDNY’s 
6 
MGP sites?  
7 
A. 
While KEDNY has made progress at most of its MGP sites, SIR spending 
8 
above the Historic Test Year level is expected to continue through the Rate 
9 
and Data Years.  The increase in SIR costs at KEDNY’s MGP sites (i.e., 
10 
excluding Gowanus Canal and Newtown Creek) from $31.678 million in 
11 
the Historic Test Year to $58.100 million in the Rate Year is due to 
12 
increased remedial action work that KEDNY anticipates will take place in 
13 
the Rate Year.  While the Company will continue its investigation and 
14 
remediation activities at all 23 MGP sites worked on in the Historic Test 
15 
Year, approximately 90 percent of the spending forecast for the Rate Year 
16 
is due to activity at four sites.  Specifically, KEDNY will be conducting 
17 
significant remedial activities at the Citizens Gas Works, Fulton Municipal 
18 
Gas Works, Metropolitan Gas Works, and Equity Works.  Portions of the 
19 
remedial plans at three of these sites (i.e., Citizens, Fulton Municipal Gas 
20 
Works, and Metropolitan Gas Works) must be completed on a schedule 
21 


--- Page 18 ---
Testimony of Charles F. Willard 
Page 16 of 40 
coordinated with the EPA’s schedule for the Gowanus Canal remediation. 
1 
 
2 
The specific work at the Citizens Gas Works, Fulton Municipal Gas Works, 
3 
Metropolitan Gas Works, and Equity Works, along with general SIR 
4 
program activity, that will take place in the Rate Year includes the 
5 
following: 
6 
 
7 
Citizens Gas Works Site – The Citizens Site remediation of Parcels I, II and 
8 
III was completed in 2021 in accordance with the DEC-approved 100 
9 
percent Remedial Design Plan and Decision Document. Following 
10 
completion of the remediation, however, the DEC, in consultation with the 
11 
EPA, determined that the contamination identified during the remedial 
12 
construction phase was more significant than was anticipated during the 
13 
design phase and, therefore, additional remediation is required.  The extent 
14 
of the additional remediation is still undetermined but will likely include 
15 
some soil excavation and groundwater treatment during the construction of 
16 
the new development.  The Company anticipates that costs for these 
17 
activities will be incurred through the Rate Year.   
18 
 
19 
Fulton Municipal Gas Works – The EPA required that a cutoff barrier wall 
20 
be constructed to separate the site from the Gowanus Canal.  This barrier 
21 


--- Page 19 ---
Testimony of Charles F. Willard 
Page 17 of 40 
wall was completed in 2021.  Current work involves remedial support of 
1 
private owners constructing buildings on the former MGP footprint during 
2 
the Rate Year.  The remediation that New York City is expected to 
3 
perform related to the combined sewer overflow tank construction is 
4 
anticipated to run through the Rate Year and the Data Years.  The New 
5 
York City park and pool-related remedial activities are anticipated to start 
6 
in the Data Years.   
7 
 
8 
Metropolitan Gas Works - The installation of the cutoff barrier wall to 
9 
separate the site from the Gowanus Canal is anticipated to be performed 
10 
under an expedited Interim Remedial Measure and is planned during the 
11 
Rate Year. The work is expected to include remedial measures that are 
12 
protective of the Gowanus Canal  
13 
 
14 
Equity Works Site – Remedial construction is planned during the Rate Year.  
15 
Following this remediation, the site will be returned to the property owner 
16 
and future site management will fall under the site management and OM&M 
17 
plans.  
18 
  
19 
SIR Program Activities – Activities including pursuit of cost recovery from 
20 
other parties and legacy insurance policies are expected to continue along 
21

Testimony of Charles F. Willard 
Page 18 of 40 
with other SIR program activities.  
1 
 
2 
Q.  
What are the major drivers of SIR costs in the Data Years for 
3 
KEDNY’s MGP sites?  
4 
A.  
For Data Year 1, spending is forecast to remain significant, but lower than 
5 
the Rate Year, at approximately $49.618 million.  In Data Year 1, the 
6 
Citizens and Fulton Site will continue to incur significant remediation costs.  
7 
The Greenpoint Site and Williamsburg Site are forecast to have moderate 
8 
costs for investigation and site management support activities, remedial 
9 
design and remedial construction.  The Metropolitan and Equity Site costs 
10 
will decrease as construction activities are completed.   
11 
 
12 
In Data Year 2, KEDNY anticipates spending approximately $61.858 
13 
million.  This encompasses continued work at the Fulton site and increased 
14 
spending at the Williamsburg Site due to construction activities, as well as 
15 
moderate spending at the Greenpoint Site due to design, investigation and

17 
construction activities are completed.

4 
Q.  
Turning to the Gowanus Canal and Newtown Creek Superfund sites, 
5 
please provide some background on these sites.  
6 
A.  
Gowanus Canal - KEDNY had three former MGP facilities located along 
7 
the Gowanus Canal – Fulton, Citizens, and Metropolitan.  These former 
8 
MGP facilities are individual remediation projects (as discussed above) and, 
9 
along with other industries and processes located on or near the canal, have 
10 
contributed to the need for environmental remediation of the canal itself, 
11 
which is listed on the EPA’s Superfund National Priorities List.    
12 
 
13 
In 2013, the EPA identified a remediation plan for the Gowanus Canal that 
14 
involves removing contaminated sediment via dredging, installing a cap, 
15 
and other activities.  In 2014, the EPA issued a Unilateral Administrative 
16 
Order (“UAO”), provided in Exhibit __ (CFW-3), to KEDNY and 26 other 
17 
parties to perform the remedial design of the EPA’s selected remedy in 
18 
accordance with the schedule set by the EPA.  The EPA led and expedited 
19 
the completion of the remedial investigation and feasibility study in a little 
20 
more than two years.  The cleanup is being designed in multiple phases 
21 


--- Page 23 ---
Testimony of Charles F. Willard 
Page 21 of 40 
(RTAs 1,2, and 3).  Each RTA is approximately one-third of the canal 
1 
length with RTA-1 being at the head of the canal, or furthest north, and 
2 
RTA-2 in the middle, and RTA-3 at the southern extent where the canal 
3 
connects to Gowanus Bay.  The design sequence requires RTA-1 
4 
construction be completed first, followed by RTA-2, and finally, RTA-3.  
5 
   
6 
In 2019, the EPA issued UAO 2, provided in Exhibit __ (CFW-3A), to 
7 
KEDNY and the same 26 parties, ordering the parties to perform the tasks 
8 
necessary to provide structural support for bulkheads and infrastructure in 
9 
preparation for implementation of the dredging and capping remedy 
10 
selected in the EPA’s September 27, 2013 Record of Decision for the 
11 
Gowanus Canal.  An amendment to the UAO, provided in Exhibit _ 
12 
(CFW-3B), was issued in April 2019 to KEDNY and the same 26 parties, 
13 
assigning additional bulkheads and infrastructure requiring support during 
14 
the remedial construction of RTA-1.  In 2020, the EPA issued UAO 3, 
15 
provided in Exhibit __ (CFW-3C), ordering six of the original parties, 
16 
including KEDNY, to implement the remedial action in RTA-1 and procure 
17 
a site staging area for future RTA-2 dredging and capping work.  The final 
18 
design inclusive of the remedial requirements in the Record of Decision and 
19 
UAOs was completed in September 2020 and construction began in RTA-
20 
1 in November 2020 with the site mobilization.  As of December 2022, 
21 


--- Page 24 ---
Testimony of Charles F. Willard 
Page 22 of 40 
RTA-1 remedial action is underway and more than 50 percent complete.   
1 
It is important to note that at least 26 other parties are responsible for the 
2 
costs of the remedial design of the Gowanus Canal under the UAO and at 
3 
least a subset of this group has been named responsible for construction and 
4 
cleanup under the existing remedial action UAOs, and presumably, any 
5 
future EPA orders.  KEDNY has been proactive in its efforts to research 
6 
and work with the EPA to identify and engage these and other potentially 
7 
responsible parties to equitably share the cost of investigation and 
8 
remediation.   
9

Newtown Creek - At the Newtown Creek Superfund site, the remedial 
11 
investigation and feasibility study commenced in 2011 and is in progress.  
12 
This project is proceeding more slowly than Gowanus Canal because the 
13 
EPA is pursuing a traditional remedial investigation conducted by a group 
14 
of responsible parties.  KEDNY and five other responsible parties have 
15 
entered an Administrative Settlement Agreement and Order on Consent, 
16 
provided in Exhibit __ (CFW-4), with the EPA to conduct the Newtown 
17 
Creek remedial investigation and feasibility study.  The remedial 
18 
investigation of this site is still in review with the EPA as of January 2023.    
19 
The feasibility study to develop and evaluate remedial alternatives has 
20 
commenced.  However, it is likely to be some time before a remedy is 
21 


--- Page 25 ---
Testimony of Charles F. Willard 
Page 23 of 40 
evaluated, selected, and designed, and construction commences.  During 
1 
the remedy evaluation process, it is expected that some supplemental 
2 
investigation will be required.  Given the size and complexity of this site, 
3 
the remainder of the investigation, design, and construction is likely to be 
4 
lengthy, extensive, and costly.  The responsible parties have proposed an 
5 
Early Action to the EPA and are working to develop the scope and 
6 
objectives for this expedited remedial work that will target a portion of 
7 
Newtown Creek. 
8 
 
9 
KEDNY continues to be very active in discussions with the other 
10

11

12

13 
who executed the settlement agreement to develop information regarding 
14

15 
parties to enable KEDNY to seek cost recovery proportionate to its 
16

Q.  
What is KEDNY’s proposal for recovery of its SIR costs?  
17 
A. 
KEDNY proposes to include the following cost components in base rates:  
18 
(i) 
a forecast of $100 million per year in each of the Rate Year and Data 
19 
Years for the MGP sites and Gowanus Canal and Newtown Creek 
20 
SIR work; and 
21 


--- Page 28 ---
Testimony of Charles F. Willard 
Page 26 of 40 
(ii) 
the current amortization of $18.521 million annually, representing 
1 
one-tenth of the forecast deferral balance at December 31, 2016 
2 
through December 31, 2026 (“Historic Amortization”). 
3 
 
4 
While the average forecast for the MGP sites and Gowanus Canal and 
5 
Newtown Creek SIR work is $127 million per year, the Company believes 
6 
that its $100 million per year forecast is a more reasonable estimate for 
7 
inclusion in rates.  KEDNY will continue to pursue offsets to remediation 
8 
costs, including insurance recoveries, the identification of potentially 
9 
responsible parties, and property sales.  Some of these potential offsets are 
10 
expected to occur in the Rate Year and Data Years but are not known or 
11 
measurable at this time.  The forecast rate allowance accounts for these 
12 
potential future offsets in a manner intended to mitigate bill impacts. 
13 
 
14 
Because of the uncertainty and magnitude of SIR costs, KEDNY proposes 
15 
to continue the current SIR reconciliation mechanism, whereby actual SIR 
16 
expenses are reconciled to the base rate allowance (exclusive of the 
17 
amortization), with any difference deferred for future recovery from, or 
18 
credit to, customers.  The reconciliation mechanism is critical as a number 
19 
of the Company’s MGP sites are anticipated to continue to have significant 
20 
expense variability.  The provision in the current reconciliation related to 
21 


--- Page 29 ---
Testimony of Charles F. Willard 
Page 27 of 40 
the Citizen’s Site, requiring the Company to absorb ten percent of the costs, 
1 
would also continue.  KEDNY also proposes to continue the current SIR 
2 
Recovery Surcharge.  
3 
 
4 
Q.  
Why does KEDNY believe that its proposal is consistent with the 
5 
Commission’s guidance and in the best interests of customers?    
6 
A.  
The rate recovery proposal is consistent with the SIR Generic Order as it is 
7 
tailored to address the specific conditions at KEDNY – namely, the rate 
8 
allowance reflects significant forecast SIR costs combined with a means to 
9 
recover a portion of KEDNY’s historic deferral balance.  In the SIR 
10 
Generic Order, the Commission noted its intent to retain the flexibility to 
11 
“tailor the rate treatment of SIR costs to the concrete conditions of each 
12 
utility” and moderate annual bill impacts by spreading the amortization of 
13 
costs over a longer period “as needed and as dictated by the costs in specific 
14 
rate cases.”  Increasing the rate allowance for forecast MGP-related SIR 
15 
costs and including a projection of costs for Gowanus Canal and Newtown 
16 
Creek allows the Company to recover costs going forward and hopefully 
17 
avoids adding to an already substantial deferral balance.  The projected 
18 
rate allowance also factors in potential offsets to remediation costs that may 
19 
occur but are unknown at this time.  Maintaining the SIR Recovery 
20 
Surcharge enables the Company to mitigate bill impacts by not reflecting 
21 


--- Page 30 ---
Testimony of Charles F. Willard 
Page 28 of 40 
an overly aggressive forecast in base rates, while addressing the uncertainty 
1 
in forecasting.  These same circumstances justified the Commission’s 
2 
adoption of the SIR Recovery Surcharge in the 2019 KEDNY and KEDLI 
3 
Rate Cases.  The recovery proposal reflects the specific SIR cost issues 
4 
affecting the Company and is intended to avoid future rate shock to 
5 
customers. 
6 
 
7 
Q. 
What is the Company’s proposal for the Historic Amortization that 
8 
ends December 31, 2026? 
9 
A. 
The Historic Amortization was calculated based on an estimate of the SIR 
10 
deferral balance at December 31, 2016.  At December 31, 2026, KEDNY 
11 
forecasts a remaining regulatory asset of $19.188 million associated with 
12 
the Historic Amortization.  As it was a forecast, the Company will 
13 
reconcile the amount at December 31, 2016 for future disposition.  
14

APPENDIX 1 – KEDNY SIR COST CONTROL EFFORTS 
1 
2 
The Company follows the cost management practices set forth in the Inventory of 
3 
Best Practices for Utility SIR Programs established in Case 11-M-0034 and as 
4 
clarified by the New York State Department of Environmental Conservation 
5 
(“DEC”).  Examples of the Company’s SIR cost control efforts are discussed 
6 
below. 
7 
8 
Experienced Staff and Contractors 
9 
The Company staffs its SIR program with experienced project managers.  The 
10 
project managers are responsible for, among other things, monitoring project 
11 
activity, ensuring that work is performed in accordance with work plans and project 
12 
specifications, and managing project costs, including reviewing contractor 
13 
invoices.  The Company also utilizes specialized environmental consultants and 
14 
contractors to perform investigation, engineering design, remedial evaluation and 
15 
construction, construction oversight, and operations and maintenance activities in 
16 
accordance with the Company’s policies and accepted industry practices. 
17 
18 
Competitive Bidding Process 
19 
Procurement of consultants and contractors differ due to nature and scope of the 
20 
work.  The scope of work for contractor work is typically well defined in bid 
21 
Appendix 1 
Page 1 of 8 


--- Page 35 ---
Testimony of Charles F. Willard 
Page 33 of 40 
specifications based on prior field studies and approved by the DEC.  The scope 
1 
of work for consultants is defined based on the immediate project scope of work 
2 
which may expand due to the progressive nature of the work.  For instance, the 
3 
scope could change following the collection of new data and direction set by the 
4 
DEC.  As more data is collected, it is often advantageous to keep the same 
5 
consultant on the project due to reduced transaction costs and the benefit of 
6 
consultant’s site knowledge. 
7 
8 
Contractors 
9 
Contractor work relating to environmental response activities are subject to a 
10 
project-specific competitive bid selection process, with the exception of a select 
11 
number of small projects performed under Blanket Construction Contracts. 
12 
Blanket Construction Contracts are established through competitive bids for rates 
13 
and equipment and are utilized for smaller, time and material construction projects 
14 
and routine site support work at select sites.  During the project-specific 
15 
competitive bid process on larger projects, a request for proposal (“RFP”) is issued 
16 
to usually three to six pre-qualified vendors.  Final vendor selection is based on 
17 
specific criteria such as cost, technical merit, and personnel qualifications. 
18 
19 
Exceptions to these standard procedures are made in situations where it is necessary 
20 
to maintain vendor consistency through multi-phased assessment/remediation 
21 
Appendix 1 
Page 2 of 8 


--- Page 36 ---
Testimony of Charles F. Willard 
Page 34 of 40 
projects or to implement immediate or short-term response to imminent hazards 
1 
when there is not time for a competitive bid process.  For work not competitively 
2 
bid or performed under a Blanket Construction Contract, the reasons for not 
3 
competitively bidding the work are clearly documented and must be approved by 
4 
Procurement as an exception to the usual bid process. 
5 
6 
Consultants 
7 
In 2018, the Company concluded a procurement to secure competitive pricing and 
8 
terms to establish new Master Services Agreements (“MSAs”) for routine SIR 
9 
related environmental consulting work, as well as, construction management work. 
10 
Consultants were initially evaluated based on their qualifications and competitive 
11 
rates.  The Company negotiated MSAs with the consulting firms that offered the 
12 
best value for the services needed while taking advantage of the work volume 
13 
discounts afforded to National Grid.  As a result, the consulting pool was 
14 
expanded from five to seven firms providing a wider range of expertise to support 
15 
program activities. 
16 
17 
Where specialty consulting is needed, particularly in support of legal matters, 
18 
interviews are conducted and rates established with a single vendor in consideration 
19 
of the specialty services required. 
20 
21 
Appendix 1 
Page 3 of 8 


--- Page 37 ---
Testimony of Charles F. Willard 
Page 35 of 40 
Consultants provide site-specific proposals, along with Work Authorization Forms 
1 
for new assignments, followed by Change Notifications to address changes to the 
2 
scope of work.  The proposed scope is reviewed by the SIR Company Project 
3 
Manager and passed onto management for review and approval.  Because the 
4 
Company Project Manager is in frequent communication with the consultant, 
5 
receives work products and periodically observes the field work, they verify the 
6 
work completed in the monthly invoice.  A project analyst is tasked with verifying 
7 
that the invoiced items and rates are consistent with the MSA contract. 
8 
9 
Invoice Review 
10 
Vendors must prepare detailed monthly invoices that include the following 
11 
information: 
12 
•
a project description that meets applicable regulatory requirements and
13 
identifies the purchase order number and work authorization number;
14 
15 
•
the budget for each task, the amount invoiced for each task on the current
16 
invoice, the total amount invoiced to date, and the amount remaining in the
17 
purchase order or work authorization for completion of the task;
18 
19 
•
backup to the invoice that contains a detailed breakdown of costs for each
20 
task must be attached to each monthly invoice. Required details include
21 
Appendix 1 
Page 4 of 8 


--- Page 38 ---
Testimony of Charles F. Willard 
Page 36 of 40 
where appropriate labor charges broken down by employee name, labor 
1 
category, billable rate, current and cumulative hours worked on the task, 
2 
current and cumulative dollars billed, and a breakdown of other direct 
3 
charges; and 
4 
5 
•
for lengthy projects, the vendor must submit a monthly progress report that
6 
identifies, by task, the activities completed in the period, the task budget,
7 
the amount expended during the current billing period, and the total amount
8 
expended to date. The vendor must also estimate the percent completion of
9 
each task.
10 
11 
In addition, contractor invoices are reviewed by comparing the summary and 
12 
detailed invoices to the original bid spreadsheet to determine if the vendor’s level 
13 
of effort corresponds to the bid estimate.  Special attention is directed to those 
14 
tasks where the level of effort and/or the cost exceeds the estimate.  In such cases, 
15 
contractors are required to submit change order requests with revised cost detail. 
16 
Following SIR Project Manager review and, if appropriate, acceptance of all or a 
17 
portion of the proposed increase, the contractor updates the spreadsheet with the 
18 
approved change. 
19 
20 
Appendix 1 
Page 5 of 8 


--- Page 39 ---
Testimony of Charles F. Willard 
Page 37 of 40 
Cost Effective Alternatives 
1 
Although the Company has limited control over the scope and timing of its SIR 
2 
activities, it does manage costs in the areas it can control, such as contracting 
3 
procedures, and challenges the DEC and the United States Environmental 
4 
Protection Agency (“EPA”) when a more cost effective and equally protective 
5 
remedy is available.  The Company manages its SIR programs to eliminate risk to 
6 
human health and the environment in a cost effective manner, and works closely 
7 
with the environmental regulatory agencies to accomplish this goal.  Examples of 
8 
instances where the Company was successful in developing and receiving approval 
9 
for more cost-effective alternatives than the DEC’s initial remedy decisions are 
10 
provided in Exhibit __ (CFW-5). 
11 
12 
In addition, to ensure appropriate disposal of materials from all MGP sites, the 
13 
Company uses only facilities approved by the National Grid Vendor Advisory 
14 
Group, a cross-functional group comprised of National Grid employees from the 
15 
SIR, Environmental Compliance, Legal and Procurement Departments.  The 
16 
group employs a pre-qualification and auditing process that reduces the likelihood 
17 
that disposal facilities themselves ultimately become the subject of future 
18 
environmental proceedings.

Outreach Efforts 
1 
The Company provides MGP site progress reports to the DEC and EPA on a 
2 
quarterly, and monthly basis, respectively.  The Company also participates in 
3 
outreach to local communities and officials, citizen groups, and property owners, 
4 
and established public websites for major MGP sites that contain information about 
5 
the sites and the respective investigation and remediation activities.  The 
6 
Company strongly believes that purposeful community outreach efforts are critical 
7 
to a successful remediation effort. 
8 
9 
Insurance Cost Recovery 
10 
The Company attempts to reduce its SIR cost responsibility by seeking recoveries 
11 
from insurers and other responsible third parties.  Efforts to recover outstanding 
12 
insurance claims have involved a combination of litigation and settlement activities 
13 
associated with the Company’s SIR sites.  To date, the Company has settled its 
14 
MGP claims with a number of insurance companies for approximately $69.8 
15 
million.  The Company is also engaged in litigation with other insurers. 
16 
17 
Identification of Other PRPs 
18 
The Company seeks aggressively to contribute only its equitable share of remedial 
19 
costs.  For example, the Company filed a federal Superfund lawsuit in the Eastern 
20 
District of New York against 15 parties seeking cost recovery and contribution for 
21 
Appendix 1 
Page 7 of 8 


--- Page 41 ---
Testimony of Charles F. Willard 
Page 39 of 40 
environmental remediation costs in the area of the former Williamsburg MGP site.  
1 
The Company uses a similar strategy to seek other responsible parties at a number 
2 
of its former MGP sites. The Company also works collaboratively, where possible, 
3 
with property owners to conduct the construction phases of SIR projects during 
4 
property redevelopment or construction activities to minimize the overall 
5 
contribution of costs. This approach provides opportunities for synergies that could 
6 
yield cost savings for all parties. 
7 
8 
In addition, the Company has taken a proactive approach to ensure that it pays only 
9 
an equitable share of the remediation costs at Gowanus Canal and Newtown Creek, 
10 
two federal Superfund sites with multiple responsible parties.  The Company has 
11 
performed research to identify potentially responsible parties, worked with both 
12 
EPA and the cooperative parties to help ensure equitable participation in the 
13 
projects, and worked through a mediation process to establish liability shares for 
14 
site-related costs.

SITE INFORMATION AND PROGRESS SUMMARY
General Site Information
Site Name:
Bay Ridge Holder Stations A & B
NYSDEC Site ID:
224058
Site location:
A: 837 64th Street, Brooklyn, NY; B: 829-884 65th Street, Brooklyn, NY
PRP:
NA
Site Background
The Site is a former holder station located on two non-contiguous parcels in a primarily commercial area of 
Brooklyn, NY. Station A, or OU-1, is non-owned and is currently a parking lot for an adjacent supermarket.  
Station B, or OU-2, is owned by National Grid. Station B contains little league ball fields, the National Grid Bay 
Ridge gate station, and a currently vacant area. 
OU-1 requires no further action as stated in the February 2012 SC Report approval letter. NYSDEC requested an 
Interim SMP be prepared and an environmental easement be used to restrict the future uses of the gate station 
area and an adjacent vacant area of Station B (OU-2). 
Site Investigation and Remediation Progress January 2019 through December 2022
Per direction of NYSDEC, annual groundwater monitoring is on-going. The emerging contaminants groundwater 
sampling summary report was submitted to NYSDEC in February 2019.  Sampling and monitoring support for 
National Grid’s capital improvement project commenced in 2019. A NOIA regarding a capital improvement project 
was submitted to NYSDEC in March 2019. An intrusive activity summary report was submitted to NYSDEC in June 
2019.
The Interim SMP was revised in April 2020 and approved in October 2021. The property was re-surveyed for the 
Interim SMP Environmental Easement in November 2020. A recommendation to reduce groundwater sampling 
was submitted to NYSDEC in the 2020 Annual Groundwater Summary Report in February 2021 and approved in 
March 2021 from annual to biennial.  A soil import form for the vacant portion of the property was submitted to 
NYSDEC in May 2021. Import request forms were submitted to NYSDEC in March 2022.

SITE INFORMATION AND PROGRESS SUMMARY
General Site Information
Site Name:
Citizens Gas Works MGP Site  
NYSDEC Site ID:
C224012
Site location:
Smith Street and Luquer Street, Brooklyn, NY
PRP:
NA
Site Background
Citizens MGP Site, also referred to as the Carroll Gardens Site, is an 11-acre former MGP site located in Brooklyn, 
NY on the Gowanus Canal. The site OU-1 parcels are owned by a 3rd party and NYC. The NYC owned property 
included a concrete production facility operated by a 3rd party which stopped operating in 2018. The off-site 
areas currently subject to remedial investigation are referred to as OU-2. The site was under the VCA until 2007 
when it became part of the NYS BCP with the other property owners to accelerate redevelopment of the site after 
the MGP remediation. The property is mostly vacant and surrounded by commercial and residential properties 
and the Gowanus Canal which is under the federal superfund program. National Grid previously received 
information regarding properties within OU-2 that may be subject to redevelopment in the future. The 
redevelopment of Parcel 3 is expected to start in 2023, and potentially in 2024 for Parcel 1.   
The RDD for OU-1 was issued by NYSDEC in April 2007. In March 2012, the NYSDEC requested and approved the 
commencement of a DNAPL O&M program to collect DNAPL at the site.
Site Investigation and Remediation Progress January 2019 through December 2022
Addenda No 1 and 2 to the NYSDEC-approved 100% Design regarding changes to some of the bulkhead tie-
backs was submitted to NYSDEC in February 2019. Addendums numbered 5 and 6 to the RD were submitted to 
NYSDEC in July and August 2021, respectively. A Data Summary Report for Parcel IV was submitted to NYSDEC 
in August 2021. The Supplemental Soil Investigation Work Plan for Parcel IV was submitted to NYSDEC in 
October 2021. A Baseline Groundwater Monitoring Work Plan was submitted to NYSDEC in December 2021. A 
Data Summary report for OU-2 offsite areas was submitted to NYSDEC in November 2021.
National Grid worked with NYC to coordinate the Site remediation schedule and the timing of the City’s former 
tenant completing the full demobilization of their former concrete production facility in the Summer of 2019. 
NYSDEC issued a fact sheet in May 2019 and held an availability session in June 2019 to discuss the start of the 
remediation project at the site. National Grid contactors mobilized to the site and initiated the remedial 
construction activities for Parcels I, II, and III in July 2019. Remedial activities were completed in Q1 2022. Site 
restoration activities were completed in Q2 2022.
The DNAPL recovery program continued until June 2019 when it was suspended in anticipation of the remedial 
construction efforts and removal of the concrete pile/debris. The installation of new piezometers and recovery 
wells at OU-1 on Parcels II and III of the Site was completed in Q1 2022. The DNAPL recovery program resumed 
for OU-1 in Q1 2022. 
Coordination with the Parcel IV property owner to obtain access for the implementation of the NYSDEC-approved 
supplemental soil investigation occurred throughout 2022. In Q1 and 2 2022, monitoring wells were installed, 
existing wells were developed, and wells were sampled in accordance with the Baseline Groundwater Monitoring 
Work Plan. The draft Interim SMP for Parcels I and II of the Site was submitted to NYSDEC in April 2022. The 
NYSDEC-requested survey and available gauging data for Site-related wells were submitted to NYSDEC in May 
2022. The Baseline Groundwater Monitoring Report was submitted to NYSDEC in May 2022. The Supplemental 
Off-Site Investigation Work Plan for OU-2 was submitted to NYSDEC in August 2022. Semi-annual post-
remediation groundwater sampling commenced in October 2022. The NYSDEC-approved supplemental off-Site 
investigation field activities for OU-2 were initiated in October 2022. Groundwater samples were collected from 
newly-installed off-Site groundwater monitoring wells in December 2022.

The MGP site is located on Staten Island. The site is divided into two OUs. OU-1 is located at 40 Willow Avenue 
and is the location of a former relief holder and has a gas regulator station operating on part of it. The OU-2 is

compliance with the ROD and RD.
Site Investigation and Remediation Progress January 2019 through December 2022
Oversight and CAMP monitoring of geotechnical borings for NYCDDC for upcoming sewer and water utility 
installation in Willow Avenue was started in June 2019. A summary report regarding the NYCDDC geotechnical

utility installation project are ongoing. A NOIA regarding the National Grid Gas Operations project was submitted 
to NYSDEC in April 2020 and CAMP, oversight, and soil and groundwater disposal were provided during this work 
and was completed March 2021
SMP reporting, NAPL monitoring and recovery, and groundwater sampling and management is ongoing.  Semi-
annual reports are submitted to NYSDEC in January and July. Operations, monitoring and sampling of the 
Groundwater Treatment System for the Containment Cell is ongoing. The emerging contaminants groundwater 
sampling summary report was submitted to the NYSDEC in February 2019.  During Q4 2022, three piezometers 
and two vapor intrusion sampling points were abandoned at an offsite property.

SITE INFORMATION AND PROGRESS SUMMARY
General Site Information
Site Name:
Coney Island MGP Site (Brooklyn Borough Gas 
Works)
NYSDEC Site ID:
224026
Site location:
Shell Road and Shore Parkway, Brooklyn, NY 
PRP:
NA
Site Background
The site is a former MGP site located on Neptune Avenue and Shore Parkway, Brooklyn, Coney Island New York. 
The property is currently owned by National Grid and two small parcels are owned by the NYCT. OU-1 ROD to 
install barrier wall around the perimeter of the site was issued in March 2001. The OU-2 ROD to remediate the 
creek and uplands impacted areas was issued in March 2002 and was subsequently split into OU-2 and OU-3, - 
respectively. The adjacent properties are primarily commercial/residential. There are no active utility operations 
on the property.
The OU-1 barrier wall installation was completed in 2004. The OU-2 creek dredge and OU-3 uplands excavation 
remediation were completed in November 2008. In 2010, the NYSDEC approved the SMP governing O&M 
activities at the site which includes gauging and sampling select wells, measuring the engineered cap thickness in 
the creek and confirming the uplands engineered cap system and ecological buffer are intact. In February 2012, 
the NYSDEC issued an Explanation of Significant Difference removing the groundwater and NAPL collection and 
treatment system components from the O&M activities after National Grid proved that NAPL recovery via 
automated system was not feasible. In June 2012, the Environmental Notice for the site was recorded with the 
NYC Registrar’s Office.
Site Investigation and Remediation Progress January 2019 through December 2022
The emerging contaminants groundwater sampling report was submitted to the NYSDEC in February 2019. A 
letter from NYSDEC was received June 2020 that the sampling for the site has been satisfied and no further 
sampling is required at this time.  A recovery well installation and monitoring well abandonment request letter 
was submitted to NYSDEC in March 2019. New recovery wells were installed in locations with accumulating 
DNAPL and monitoring wells were abandoned in September 2019. A work plan for the abandonment and re-
installation of two damaged monitoring wells was submitted to NYSDEC in September 2019 and performed in 
December 2019.Currently, O&M in accordance with the approved SMP (Revision 5, June 2019) is being performed 
on a periodic basis. Periodic review reports for O&M activities are submitted to NYSDEC annually. Semi-annually 
sampling was reduced to annual sampling in 2020 in accordance with the SMP.A letter requesting the 
postponement of additional evaluation of scouring in Coney Island Creek was submitted to NYSDEC in March 
2019. NOIAs regarding intrusive NYCDDC projects were submitted to NYSDEC in April 2019 and March 2021. A 
NOIA regarding Coney Island Creek sampling conducted by the USEPA was submitted to NYSDEC in April 2021.To 
support upcoming construction activities to be performed by NYCDDC and the MTA, a work plan for a boring 
program within the Coney Island Creek was submitted and approved by NYSDEC in April 2019.  The borings were 
conducted in June 2019. A summary report for the borings was submitted in July 2019. Several monitoring wells 
were modified from stick-up to flush mount prior to the start of the MTA project. Environmental oversight and 
CAMP monitoring of intrusive activities for the MTA project began in September 2019 and was completed in 2021. 
A summary report for the CAMP monitoring of NYCDDC projects was submitted in December 2021.
A work plan for the abandonment of recovery wells and an evaluation of the passive vent system were submitted 
to NYSDEC in May 2022 and a revised work plan was submitted to NYSDEC in August 2022. A NOIA was 
submitted to NYSDEC regarding investigation of the property related to due diligence in September 2022. In Q4 
2022, the passive vent system evaluation was initiated, and recovery wells were abandoned, as approved by 
NYSDEC; the passive vent system evaluation is ongoing.

Site Name:
Equity Works MGP Site
NYSDEC Site ID:
224050
Site location:

PRP:
NA
Site Background
The approximately 2-acre site, situated on three non-owned properties, was a former MGP and is located in an 
industrial area of Greenpoint, Brooklyn, NY.    
The 222 Maspeth Ave property has been operated as a waste transfer station by Cooper Tank Recycling, which 
has been permitted to expand their waste transfer operations onto the 252 and 254 Maspeth Ave properties.  In 
late 2017 Cooper Tank moved their transfer station to another location.  They still own parcels 222 and 254 and 
maintain the NYSDEC permit to operate a transfer station.  However, they no longer control parcel 252, which is 
currently being used for bus parking. 
The site is in the FS phase. However, NYSDEC has required supplemental investigation in advance of the FS being 
approved. An interim SMP has been prepared and approved by NYSDEC in December 2012. A soil removal IRM 
was completed in 2012 and DNAPL recovery system installed in 2015. 
Site Investigation and Remediation Progress January 2019 through December 2022
The final Supplemental Investigation Report for the 222 Maspeth Avenue parcel was submitted in March 2019. 
Quarterly DNAPL gauging and recovery activities are ongoing. Summary Reports for the NAPL Recovery Collection 
IRM are submitted to NYSDEC annually.
 
A PDI Work Plan and RD Work Plan for an IRM was submitted to the NYSDEC in April 2019. The PDI work was 
completed in May and June 2019. The IRM work plan was re-submitted to the NYSDEC in December 2019. In 
December 2019, NYSDEC, National Grid, and the property owner conducted a conference call to resolve how the 
property owner would participate in the IRM design process. Revised IRM Work Plans were submitted in January 
and April 2020. NYSDEC submitted a property owner’s comments to the revised IRM Work Plan in February 2020. 
In March 2020, NYSDEC, National Grid, and a property owner conducted conference calls to resolve how to 
address NYSDEC’s February 2020 request to install two new monitoring wells and to complete groundwater 
monitoring following completion of the IRM work. The Final IRM Work Plan was submitted in June 2020. An IRM 
WP Addendum was submitted to NYSDEC in July 2021 and implementation commenced in August 2021. The 60% 
design for the IRM was submitted to NYSDEC in October 2020 and the 90% design was submitted in July 2021.  
The IRM 95% Design in March 2022.  In Q2 2022, a bid walk with remedial contractors was completed to support 
contractor bids for the IRM.  The 100% 222 Maspeth Avenue IRM design was submitted to and approved by the 
NYSDEC in August 2022.
Throughout 2022, weekly gauging and NAPL recovery from pilot recovery wells were performed, as outlined in 
the 222 Maspeth Avenue IRM Work Plan Addendum. The first quarterly DNAPL recovery O&M event was 
completed in January 2022, and quarterly O&M events were conducted throughout the year. Upgrades to the 
automated DNAPL recovery system were completed in November 2022. NYSDEC approved modifications to the 
frequency of DNAPL gauging and recovery at select pilot recovery wells originally outlined in the IRM Work Plan 
Addendum in November 2022.

was completed in October 2021.
The draft RA Work Plan and the Waste Characterization Work Plan were submitted in January 2022. During Q1

preparation of the RA Work Plan. The remediation of Holder No. 5, CAMP and ISMP oversight of intrusive activity 
at 218 Front St/Holder No. 4 commenced in Q4 2022. The Daily Field Report and CAMP reports related to Holder

NYSDEC by the property owner in October and November 2021, respectively. Performance of the additional Soil 
Characterization work at Parcel 3 was initiated in Q3, 2021 and is ongoing.  Access negotiations for Fulton Barrier

General Site Information
Site Name:
Gowanus Canal 
NYSDEC Site ID:
USEPA ID: 
CERCLA 02-2009-
2010
Site location:
1.8 Mile NYC man-made canal between Butler Street and Hamilton 
Avenue Bridge in Brooklyn, NY  
PRP:
Yes 
Site Background
The Gowanus Canal is located in Brooklyn. Three MGP sites are located on the Gowanus Canal. The MGP sites are 
subject to an Order on Consent with the NYSDEC. The USEPA has included the Gowanus Canal on the National 
Priorities List and has completed RI and FS for the site. USEPA issued a ROD for the Site in September 2013. 
National Grid is participating in the performance of the remedial design with other responsible parties under a 
UAO with the USEPA. 
Site Investigation and Remediation Progress January 2019 through December 2022
The site is under USEPA Superfund Program and not under NYSDEC Division of Environmental Remediation’s 
Program.  Due to technical complexities of managing a project of this scale and managing expectations of third-
party stakeholders and the USEPA, multiple experts were contracted to review the approach and ensure 
compliance with CERCLA regulations. The Gowanus Remediation Team continues to develop the RD plans for the 
project. In September 2019, the 90% RD for RTA-1 was submitted to USEPA for review.  Major elements were 
accepted by USEPA December 2019.  In February and March 2020, the final RTA-1 and Turning Basin 1 remedial 
designs were submitted to USEPA.  RTA-1 mobilization began in September 2020 and on-water construction 
began in October 2020 with Phase 1 dredging commencing on November 16, 2020.  Through December 2022, 
dredging (Phase 1 through 3), bulkhead installation, permanent submarine cable installation at Union Street 
Bridge, and ISS activities have been completed.   USEPA approved the 100% RD for the RTA-1 work in October 
2022.  Equipment and material mobilization to support capping activities started in November 2022.

SITE INFORMATION AND PROGRESS SUMMARY
General Site Information
Site name:
Newtown Creek Superfund Site
NYSDEC Site ID:
CERCLA-02-2011-
2011
Site location:
Newtown Creek borders Brooklyn and Queens in New York City
PRP:
Y 
Site Background
Scope of SIR Work for the Site:
Newtown Creek is a 3.8-mile waterbody located on the border of Brooklyn and Queens in New York City. Two 
former MGP sites and a former gas holder are located in the vicinity of Newtown Creek. National Grid is a 
Potentially Responsible Party for addressing impacted sediments along with various oil companies, Phelps Dodge 
and NYC.  
Site Investigation and Remediation Progress January 2019 through December 2022
The site is under USEPA Superfund Program and not under NYSDEC Division of Environmental Remediation’s 
Program. The RI/FS for the entire Newtown Creek site commenced in 2011 and is scheduled for completion in 
2027. The RI/FS will: a) evaluate the nature and extent of any identified contamination, b) identify potential 
pathways of contaminant migration and possible receptors, c) develop quantitative ecological and human health 
risk assessments and, d) develop and evaluate remedial alternatives and assess whether they will meet proposed 
remedial goals and remedial action objectives. 
Drafts of the RI report were submitted by the NCG to EPA in April 2019, July 2020, October 2021, and December 
2022 with comments received from USEPA after each draft. FS field study evaluation reports were prepared and 
revised. Field work including NAPL delineation refinement and mobility sampling, groundwater seepage rate 
measurements, geotechnical coring, biota tissue sampling, nearshore sediment sampling, and definitive tests for 
ebullition-mediated NAPL transport were completed. Initial risk-based preliminary remediation goals (PRGs) were 
presented to USEPA in December 2019 and were finalized and approved in December 2021. In addition to the 
OU-1 RI/FS, an AOC was entered into voluntarily by USEPA and the NCG in 2019 to prepare a focused FS to 
evaluate the potential for an interim RA for the lower two miles of Newtown Creek which is now formally 
designated as OU-3 of the Site. A focused FS for OU-3 was prepared in 2020, but USEPA ultimately decided to 
not move forward with this early RA and terminated the AOC for OU-3 in July 2021. Also, a treatability study was 
initiated in 2019 to support the screening and evaluation of various remedial technologies for the FS, but this 
work was put on hold in 2022 in lieu of another interim RA project in East Branch. Planning for the East Branch 
Early Action was initiated in 2022.
Exhibit__(CFW-1)

The emerging contaminant groundwater analytical results summary report was submitted to NYSDEC in February 
2019. A No Further Action Response to the report was received in 2020. The ROD for OU 2 was released in 
February 2019.  The RSOI Work Plan was submitted to NYSDEC in September 2021 and approved in December 
2021. Annual Interim SMP activities, including site inspections and groundwater monitoring, are on-going.
Environmental Easements are required by the RODs. The OU 1 and OU 2 easements, including the NYSDEC’s 
suggested updated language, were sent to the owners in January 2019.  The owner’s signature is still needed to 
complete and file the OU 1 environmental easement.  NYSDEC support will be required for execution of the OU 1 
easement. A copy of the OU 2 easement, signed by the owner, was sent to NYSDEC in March 2019 for final 
approval. The OU 2 Environmental Easement was recorded in March 2020.

1. 
A. 
The New York State Department of Environmental Conservation ("Department") 
is responsible for inactive hazardous waste disposal site remedial programs pursuant to Article 
27, Title 13 of the Environmental Conservation Law ("ECL") and may issue orders consistent 
with the authority granted to the Commissioner by such statute. 
B. 
The Department is responsible for carrying out the policy of the State of New 
York to conserve, improve and protect its natural resources and environment and control water, 
land, and air pollution consistent with the authority granted to the Department and the 
Commissioner by Article 3, Title 1 of the ECL. 
C. 
This Order on Consent and Administrative Settlement ("Order & Settlement 
Agreement") is issued pursuant to the Department's authority under, inter alia, ECL Article 27, 
Title 13, ECL Article 71, Title 27, and ECL § 3-0301, and resolves Respondent's liability to the 
State under the "Comprehensive Environmental Response, Compensation, and Liability Act of 
1980", as amended ("CERCLA"), 42 U.S.C. § 9601 et seq., to the extent set forth herein. It is the 
intent of the Department and KeySpan Energy Delivery New York and KeySpan Energy Delivery 
Long Island (together and individually "KeySpan" or the "Respondent") that this Order & 
Settlement Agreement constitutes an administrative settlement within the meaning of CERCLA § 
113(f)(2),§ 113(f)(3)(B), 42 U.S.C. § 9613(f)(2), § 9613(f)(3)(B) and is a settlement under, inter 
alia, ECL Article 27, Title 13, ECL Article 71, Title 27, and ECL § 3-0301. Accordingly, 
pursuant to CERCLA § 113 (f)(3)(B), 42 U.S.c. § 9613(f)(3)(B), Respondent may seek

acceptable, fair, reasonable, and in the public interest.

II. 
Development, Perfonnance, and Reporting of Work Plans 
A. 
Work Plans 
All activities at any Site that comprise any element of an Inactive Hazardous Waste 
Disposal Site Remedial Program shall be conducted pursuant to one or more Department-
approved work plans ("Work Plan" or "Work Plans") for each Site and this Order & Settlement 
Agreement, and all activities shall be consistent with the National Oil and Hazardous Substances 
Pollution Contingency Plan ("NCP"), 40 C.F.R. Part 300, as required under CERCLA, 42 U.S.C. 
§ 9600 et seq. The Work Planes) under this Order & Settlement Agreement shall address both 
on-Site and off-Site conditions and shall be developed and implemented in accordance with 6 
NYCRR Part 375, "DER-10, Technical Guidance for Site Investigation and Remediation," 
and/or Exhibits G, H, I, J, and K of this Order & Settlement Agreement. All Department-
approved Work Plans shall be incorporated into and become enforceable parts of this Order & 
Settlement Agreement and shall be attached as Exhibit "B." Upon approval ofa Work Plan by 
the Department, Respondent shall implement such Work Plan in accordance with the schedule 
contained in such Work Plan. Nothing in this Subparagraph shall mandate that any particular 
Work Plan be submitted. 
Each Work Plan submitted for each Site shall use one of the following captions on the 
cover page: 
1. 
"Site Characterization Work Plan" ("SC Work Plan"): a Work Plan whose 
objective is to identify the presence of any hazardous waste disposed of at the Site. Such Work 
Plan shall be developed in accordance with Exhibit "G"; 
2. 
"Remedial InvestigationlFeasibility Study Work Plan" ("RIlFS Work 
Plan"): a Work Plan whose objective is to perfonn a Remedial Investigation and a Feasibility 
Study. Such Work Plan shall be developed and implemented in accordance with Exhibit "H"; 
3. 
"Interim Remedial Measure Work Plan" ("IRM Work Plan"): a Work Plan 
whose obj ective is to provide for an Interim Remedial Measure. Such Work Plan shall be 
developed in accordance with Exhibit "I"; 
4. 
"Remedial DesignlRemedial Action Work Plan" ("RDIRA Work Plan"): a 
Work Plan whose objective is to provide for the development and implementation of final plans 
and specifications for implementing the remedial alternative set forth in the ROD. Such Work 
Plan shall be developed in accordance with Exhibit "J"; or

other expert as the Department may find acceptable and shall contain a certification under 
penalty of peIjury that any institutional and/or engineering controls required by this Order & 
Settlement Agreement are unchanged from the previous certification and that nothing has 
occurred that would impair the effectiveness of such control or constitute a violation of or failure 
to comply with the approved SM Work Plan. Respondent or owner of the Site may petition the 
Department for a determination that the institutional and/or engineering controls may be 
terminated. Such petition must be supported by a statement by a Professional Engineer that such 
controls are no longer necessary for the protection of public health and the environment. The 
Department shall not unreasonably withhold its approval of such petition.

Department's Issuance ofa ROD

G. 
Release and Covenant Not to Sue 
Upon (i) the Department's approval of either the RD/RA Work Plan final report or an 
IRM Work Plan final report evidencing that no further remedial action (other than site 
management activities) is required to meet the goals of the Remedial Program for a Site, and (ii) 
the Department's acceptance of any environmental easement required pursuant to Paragraph X, 
then, except for the provisions of Paragraphs VI and VIII, and except for the future site 
management of the Site and any Natural Resource Damage claims, such acceptance shall 
constitute a release and covenant not to sue with respect to the Site for each and every claim, 
demand, remedy, or action whatsoever against Respondent, its directors, officers, employees, 
agents, servants, successors, and assigns (except successors and assigns who were responsible 
under law for the development and implementation of a Remedial Program at the Site prior to the 
effective date of this Order & Settlement Agreement), and their respective secured creditors, 
which the Department has or may have pursuant to Article 27, Title 13 of the ECL or pursuant to 
any other provision of State or Federal statutory or common law, including but not limited to § 
8 
Exhibit __ (CFW-2) 
8 of 68


--- Page 79 ---
107(a) ofCERCLA, 42 U.S.C. § 9607(a), involving or relating to investigative or remedial 
activities relative to or arising from the disposal of hazardous wastes (or other contaminants 
remediated by Respondent to the Department's satisfaction pursuant to the ROD or Work Plans) 
at the Site; provided, however, that the Department specifically reserves all of its rights 
concerning, and any such release and covenant not to sue shall not extend to any further 
investigation or remediation the Department deems necessary due to newly discovered 
environmental conditions on-Site or off-Site which are related to the disposal of hazardous 
wastes at the Site and which indicate that the Remedial Program is not protective of public health 
and/or the environment. The Department shall notify Respondent in writing of such 
environmental conditions or information and its basis for determining that the Remedial Program 
is not protective of public health and/or the environment. 
This release and covenant not to sue shall be null and void, ab initio, in the event of fraud 
relating to the execution or implementation of this Order & Settlement Agreement or in the event 
of Respondent's failure to materially comply with any provision of this Order & Settlement 
Agreement subsequent to issuance of a release and covenant not to sue, in each case with respect 
to that particular Site. The Department's determination that Respondent has committed fraud or 
has materially failed to comply with this Order & Settlement Agreement shall be subject to 
dispute resolution pursuant to Paragraph XII. 
Nothing herein shall be construed as barring, diminishing, adjudicating, or in any way 
affecting any legal or equitable rights or claims, actions, suits, causes of action, or demands 
whatsoever that (i) Respondent may have against anyone other than the Department, including 
but not limited to rights of contribution under § 113(f)(3)(B) of CERCLA, 42 U.S.c. § 
9613(f)(3)(B), and (ii) the Department may have against anyone other than Respondent, its 
directors, officers, employees, agents, and servants, and those successors and assigns of 
Respondent who were not responsible under law for the development and implementation of a 
Remedial Program at the Site prior to the effective date of this Order & Settlement Agreement, 
and their respective secured creditors.

IV. 
Penalties 
A. 
1. 
Respondent's failure to comply with any term of this Order & Settlement 
Agreement constitutes a violation of this Order & Settlement Agreement, the ECL, and 6 
NYCRR Part 375. Nothing herein abridges Respondent's right to contest any allegation that it 
has failed to comply with this Order & Settlement Agreement. 
2. 
Payment of any penalties shall not in any way alter Respondent's 
obligations under this Order & Settlement Agreement. 
B. 
1. 
Respondent shall not suffer any penalty or be subject to any proceeding or 
action in the event it cannot comply with any requirement of this Order & Settlement Agreement 
as a result of any event arising from causes beyond the reasonable control of Respondent, of any 
entity controlled by Respondent, and of Respondent's contractors, that delays or prevents the 
performance of any obligation under this Order & Settlement Agreement despite Respondent's 
best efforts to fulfill the obligation ("Force Majeure Event"). The requirement that Respondent 
exercise best efforts to fulfill the obligation includes using best efforts to anticipate the potential 
Force Majeure Event, best efforts to address any such event as it is occurring, and best efforts 
following the Force Majeure Event to minimize delay to the greatest extent possible. "Force 
Majeure" does not include Respondent's economic inability to comply with any obligation, the 
failure of Respondent to make complete and timely application for any required approval or 
permit, and non-attainment of the goals, standards, and requirements of this Order & Settlement 
Agreement. 
2. 
Respondent shall notify the Department in writing within seven (7) Days 
after it obtains knowledge of any Force Majeure Event. Respondent shall include in such notice 
the measures taken and to be taken to prevent or minimize any delays and shall request an 
appropriate extension or modification of this Order & Settlement Agreement. Failure to give 
such notice within such seven (7) Day period constitutes a waiver of any claim that a delay is not 
subject to penalties. Respondent shall be deemed to know of any circumstance which it, any 
entity controlled by it, or its contractors knew or should have known. 
3. 
Respondent shall have the burden of proving by a preponderance of the 
evidence that (i) the delay or anticipated delay has been or will be caused by a Force Majeure 
Event; (ii) the duration of the delay or the extension sought warranted under the circumstances; 
(iii) best efforts were exercised to avoid and mitigate the effects ofthe delay; and (iv) 
Respondent complied with the requirements of Subparagraph IV.B.2 regarding timely 
notification. 
10 
Exhibit __ (CFW-2) 
10 of 68


--- Page 81 ---
4. 
If the Department agrees that the delay or anticipated delay is attributable 
to a Force Majeure Event, the time for performance of the obligations that are affected by the 
Force Majeure Event shall be extended for such time as is reasonably necessary to complete 
those obligations. 
5. 
If the Department rejects Respondent's assertion that an event provides a 
defense to non-compliance with this Order & Settlement Agreement pursuant to Subparagraph 
N .B, Respondent shall be in violation of this Order & Settlement Agreement unless it invokes 
dispute resolution pursuant to Paragraph XII and Respondent's position prevails. 
V. 
Entry upon Site 
A. 
Respondent hereby consents, upon reasonable notice under the circumstances 
presented, to entry upon the Site (or areas in the vicinity of the Site which may be under the 
control of Respondent) by any duly designated officer or employee of the Department or any 
State agency having jurisdiction with respect to matters addressed pursuant to this Order & 
Settlement Agreement, and by any agent, consultant, contractor, or other person so authorized by 
the Commissioner, all of whom shall abide by the health and safety rules in effect for the Site, for 
inspecting, sampling, copying records related to the contamination at the Site, testing. and any 
other activities necessary to ensure Respondent's compliance with this Order & Settlement 
Agreement. Upon request, Respondent shall (i) provide the Department with suitable office 
space at each of the Sites, including access to a telephone, to the extent available, and (ii) permit 
the Department full access to all non-privileged records relating to matters addressed by this 
Order & Settlement Agreement. Raw data is not considered privileged and that portion of any 
privileged document containing raw data must be provided to the Department. In the event 
Respondent is unable to obtain any authorization from third-party property owners necessary to 
perform its obligations under this Order & Settlement Agreement, the Department may, 
consistent with its legal authority, assist in obtaining such authorizations. 
B. 
The Department shall have the right to take its own samples and scientific 
measurements and the Department and Respondent shall each have the right to obtain split 
samples, duplicate samples, or both, of all substances and materials sampled. The Department 
shall make the results of any such sampling and scientific measurements available to 
Respondent.

VII. 
Reservation of Rights 
A. 
Except as provided in Subparagraph lI.G, nothing contained in this Order & 
Settlement Agreement shall be construed as barring, diminishing, adjudicating, or in any way 
affecting any of the Department's rights or authorities, including, but not limited to, the right to 
require performance of further investigations and/or response action(s), to recover natural 
resource damages, and/or to exercise any summary abatement powers with respect to any person, 
including Respondent. 
B. 
Except as otherwise provided in this Order & Settlement Agreement, Respondent 
specifically reserves all rights and defenses under applicable law respecting any Departmental 
assertion of remedial liability and/or natural resource damages against Respondent, and further 
reserves all rights respecting the enforcement of this Order & Settlement Agreement, including 
the rights to notice, to be heard, to appeal, and to any other due process. The existence of this 
Order & Settlement Agreement or Respondent's compliance with it shall not be construed as an 
admission of liability, fault, wrongdoing, or breach of standard of care by Respondent, and shall 
not give rise to any presumption of law or finding of fact, or create any rights, or grant any cause 
of action, which shall inure to the benefit of any third party. Further, Respondent reserves such 
rights as it may have to seek and obtain contribution, indemnification, and/or any other form of 
recovery from its insurers and from other potentially responsible parties or their insurers for past 
or future response and/or cleanup costs or such other costs or damages arising from the 
contamination at the Site as may be provided by law, including but not limited to rights of 
contribution under § 113(f)(3)(B) of CERCLA, 42 U.S.C. § 9613(f)(3)(B). 
VIII. 
Indemnification 
Respondent shall indemnify and hold the Department, the State of New York, and their 
representatives and employees harmless for all third-party claims, suits, actions, damages, and 
costs of every name and description arising out of or resulting from the fulfillment or attempted 
fulfillment of this Order & Settlement Agreement by Respondent and/or any of Respondent's 
directors, officers, employees, servants, agents, successors, and assigns except for liability arising 
from (i) vehicular accidents occurring during travel to or from the Site; or (ii) willful, wanton, or 
malicious acts or omissions, and acts or omissions constituting gross negligence or criminal 
behavior by the Department, the State of New York, and/or their representatives and employees 
13

during the course of any activities conducted pursuant to this Order & Settlement Agreement. 
The Department shall provide Respondent with written notice no less than thirty (30) Days prior 
to commencing a lawsuit seeking indemnification pursuant to this Paragraph.

security agreement, lease, or any other right accruing to a person not affiliated with Respondent 
to secure the repayment of money or the perfonnance of a duty or obligation.
